7/25/26, 3:12 PM - Brandex: NOTICE OF OPPOSITION  

IN THE MATTER OF Application No. 735718 for registration of the trade mark "FINE PULPY" in Class 32 advertised in the Trade Marks Journal No..............


by  Opposition thereto by WAJAHAT RAMZAN, Proprietor of BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY brands (hereinafter referred to as the OPPONENT).

1. PARTICULARS OF THE OPPONENT
Name	WAJAHAT RAMZAN
Trading As	wasifa foods 

Address for Service in Pakistan	[INSERT ADVOCATE'S ADDRESS]
 PARTICULARS OF THE APPLICATION OPPOSED
Application No.	735718
Mark	FINE PULPY
Class	32
Applicant	Sheikh Khalid Bashir & Tahir Bashir, Trading as Aqsa Foods (Partners), 29-F, Block Commercial Market, Shah Rukh-e-Alam, Multan
Filing Date	29/12/2023
Goods	Beers; non-alcoholic beverages; mineral and aerated waters; fruit beverages and fruit juices; syrups and other non-alcoholic preparations for making beverages
Disclaimer	WITH THE DISCLAIMER OF "PULPY" AND ALL OTHER DESCRIPTIVE WORDS APPEARING ON THE LABEL EXCEPT AS SUBSTANTIALLY SHOWN
3. PARTICULARS OF OPPONENT'S RIGHTS
Opponent's Registered/Applied Trademark	BIG PARTY – Application No. 515608 in Class 32 (filed in 2006)
Opponent's Registered Trademark	PURE PULPY – [INSERT REGISTRATION NUMBER] in Class 32
Opponent's Copyright Registrations	2021/4718 & 2023/1878 (for PURE PULPY artistic works/labels)
Opponent's Prior Use Date	2006 onwards (continuous and extensive use)
Opponent's Goods	Beers; mineral and aerated waters and other non-alcoholic drinks; fruit drinks and fruit juices; syrups and other preparations for making beverages in Class 32
4. STATEMENT OF FACTS
That the OPPONENT, WAJAHAT RAMZAN, is engaged in the business of manufacturing, marketing, and trading of BEERS; MINERAL AND AERATED WATERS AND OTHER NON-ALCOHOLIC DRINKS; FRUIT DRINKS AND FRUIT JUICES; SYRUPS AND OTHER PREPARATIONS FOR MAKING BEVERAGES under the trade marks BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY.

That the OPPONENT is the original creator, earliest adopter, and prior user of the marks BIG PARTY (adopted in 2006) and PURE PULPY. The OPPONENT has been continuously and extensively using these marks throughout Pakistan long prior to the Applicant's filing date of 29 December 2023. In Pakistan, prior use has precedence over registration, and even unregistered trademark rights can be enforced through passing-off claims where distinctiveness has been established through continuous use. 

That the OPPONENT, in order to distinguish its goods from competitors, adopted and used the marks BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY to identify its superior quality products, which are marketed, sold, and distributed by the OPPONENT.

That to secure its intellectual property rights, the OPPONENT applied for registration of the trademark BIG PARTY under Application No. 515608 in Class 32 earliest in 2006 and has also obtained Copyright Registrations bearing Nos. 2021/4718 and 2023/1878 for its artistic works/labels, including the mark PURE PULPY. 

That since its inception in 2006, the OPPONENT's business has grown exceptionally and achieved a top ranking in the beverage industry through professionally trained workforce, commitment, and superior quality products, earning envious goodwill and recognition throughout Pakistan and beyond.

That the OPPONENT has made considerable investment in marketing, promotion, and advertising of its trademarks BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY, due to which it has acquired substantial market recognition, brand image, and customer association for goods being sold under these marks.

That the OPPONENT has immense presence throughout Pakistan due to rapid business expansion and has acquired unmatched goodwill and notoriety in the beverage sector by consistently maintaining exceptionally high standards of quality for its customers.

That the OPPONENT is also engaged in exports of its products bearing the said trademarks to various international markets, further establishing its reputation beyond the borders of Pakistan.

That it has come to the notice of the OPPONENT that the Applicant, SHEIKH KHALID BASHIR & TAHIR BASHIR, Trading as AQSA FOODS, has applied for registration of the trademark "FINE PULPY" under Application No. 735718 in Class 32.

That the Applicant's mark "FINE PULPY" is a deceptive imitation and a copy of the OPPONENT's registered copyrighted mark "PURE PULPY" as it incorporates the distinctive and dominant element "PULPY" which is the key distinctive feature of the OPPONENT's mark.

That the Applicant's mark "FINE PULPY" is visually, phonetically, and structurally deceptively similar to the OPPONENT's well-known mark "PURE PULPY" as both marks contain the word "PULPY" as the dominant and distinctive element and are used for identical goods in Class 32, thereby creating confusion and deception in the course of trade.

That the Applicant's mark "FINE PULPY" is also designed to mimic the get-up, trade dress, and overall commercial impression of the OPPONENT's PURE PULPY brand, which demonstrates the Applicant's intentional and mala fide attempt to ride upon the OPPONENT's goodwill, reputation, and market presence.

That the goods covered by the Applicant's mark and those in respect of which the OPPONENT is using its trademark PURE PULPY are identical (beverages in Class 32), which inevitably leads to confusion and deception among consumers and traders.

That the Applicant legally and factually cannot claim to be the owner or proprietor of the impugned trademark FINE PULPY, as by virtue of prior use, prior applications, and copyright registrations, the proprietorship in respect of the mark PURE PULPY vests exclusively and solely with the OPPONENT. Any claim by the Applicant to proprietorship of an identical/deceptively similar trademark is misconceived, invalid, and dishonest.

That the Applicant's application appears to have been adopted with bad intentions solely to benefit from the OPPONENT's goodwill, reputation, and market recognition. The Applicant only seeks to make easy profits at the expense, reputation, and goodwill of the OPPONENT by misleading consumers into believing that its goods originate from the OPPONENT.

That the OPPONENT is not aware of any legitimate use being made by the Applicant of the mark FINE PULPY, and any claim which the Applicant has made or intends to make is illegal, malicious, and dishonest.

That the Applicant has adopted and applied for registration of the trademark FINE PULPY in bad faith, with complete knowledge of the OPPONENT's prior rights, trademarks, and copyrights, with the ulterior motive to encroach upon the reputation and goodwill of the OPPONENT's well-known and prior mark PURE PULPY. 

That the Applicant has also filed another application No. 842408 for "BIG PULPY" , which targets the OPPONENT's BIG PARTY brand, demonstrating a clear pattern of mala fide conduct and a deliberate strategy to dilute and infringe the OPPONENT's intellectual property rights.

5. GROUNDS OF OPPOSITION
GROUND 1: Prior Use, Ownership, and Breach of Copyright (Sections 14, 17(4)(a), 17(4)(b), 18(1)(c) & 29 of the Trade Marks Ordinance, 2001 read with Section 10 of the Copyright Ordinance, 1962)
The OPPONENT is the earliest adopter, prior user, and original creator of the mark PURE PULPY. The OPPONENT holds Copyright Registrations bearing Nos. 2021/4718 and 2023/1878 for the artistic works/labels incorporating the PURE PULPY mark.  The Applicant's mark FINE PULPY is a substantial reproduction and deceptive imitation of the OPPONENT's copyrighted artistic work as it incorporates the dominant word "PULPY" . Registration would constitute a breach of copyright—a ground specifically recognized under Section 29 for opposing registration.  Under Section 17(4)(a) , earlier unregistered rights are preserved; under Section 17(4)(b) , earlier trademark applications are a bar to registration; and under Section 18(1)(c) , the OPPONENT is the earlier proprietor. Furthermore, the OPPONENT's rights are protected under common law through passing-off, as unregistered trademark rights can be enforced where distinctiveness has been acquired through continuous use.  The Applicant cannot claim ownership, and the application is liable to be refused under Section 14 (absolute grounds) as the mark is devoid of any distinctive character, with the dominant element "PULPY" merely copied from the OPPONENT's mark.

GROUND 2: Passing Off, Misappropriation of Goodwill, and Deceptive Similarity (Sections 17(4)(a), 29(4)(a), 29(4)(b), 29(3)(a), 29(3)(c), 67(1)(a) & (g) of the Trade Marks Ordinance, 2001)
The OPPONENT has acquired substantial goodwill and reputation through continuous use since 2006, nationwide sales, exports, and high-quality products under the mark PURE PULPY.  The Applicant's mark FINE PULPY is identical in goods and deceptively similar in appearance, phonetics, and structure to the OPPONENT's PURE PULPY mark, as both share the distinctive and dominant element "PULPY" . This constitutes a clear misrepresentation likely to deceive the public and cause confusion, amounting to passing off. Pakistan recognizes common law trademark rights based on prior use, enforceable through passing off.  The Applicant's action constitutes unfair and unethical business practices under Section 67(1)(a) & (g) of the Ordinance, as the Applicant seeks to pass off its low-quality products as originating from the OPPONENT or its authorized licensees, causing great loss to the OPPONENT's business and reputation. Furthermore, under Section 29, opposition can be based on grounds including lack of distinctiveness and filing in bad faith. 

GROUND 3: Bad Faith, Lack of Distinctiveness, and Dilution (Sections 14(1), 18(1)(c), 29(3)(a), 29(3)(c), 86 & 18(1)(c) of the Trade Marks Ordinance, 2001 read with Paris Convention)
The Applicant's adoption of the mark FINE PULPY is a calculated and dishonest attempt to imitate the OPPONENT's distinctive brand PURE PULPY to ride upon the OPPONENT's enormous goodwill. The Applicant filed the application with full knowledge of the OPPONENT's prior rights, as evidenced by the deceptive imitation of the OPPONENT's mark. The filing of two separate applications (FINE PULPY and BIG PULPY) targeting both of the OPPONENT's brands demonstrates a clear pattern of mala fide conduct and a systematic attempt to dilute and infringe the OPPONENT's intellectual property rights. The impugned mark is devoid of any distinctive character under Section 14(1), as it merely copies the OPPONENT's mark. The Applicant's actions smack of mala fide and dishonesty under Section 29(3)(a) & (c). The OPPONENT's mark PURE PULPY is liable to be protected as a well-known mark under Section 86 of the Ordinance read with the Paris Convention, being an earlier mark defined under Section 18(1)(c).  Registration of the impugned mark would cause dilution, deception, and confusion, and the application is therefore liable to be refused.

GROUND 4: The Disclaimer of "PULPY" Does Not Cure the Deceptive Similarity (Sections 14 & 29 of the Trade Marks Ordinance, 2001)
The Applicant has sought to disclaim the word "PULPY" . However, such disclaimer does not cure the fundamental defect of deceptive similarity, as the mark as a whole—FINE PULPY—remains confusingly similar to the OPPONENT's PURE PULPY. The disclaimer only applies to the word "PULPY" separately and apart from the mark as a whole, but the mark as a whole still incorporates the distinctive element "PULPY" which has acquired distinctiveness through the OPPONENT's extensive use. The OPPONENT can prove that the feature "PULPY" has acquired distinctiveness on account of the mark's use.  Therefore, the disclaimer cannot be used as a defence to avoid opposition.

6. PRAYER / REQUEST
In view of the foregoing facts and grounds, the OPPONENT respectfully prays that:

(a) The Application No. 735718 for registration of the trade mark "FINE PULPY" in Class 32 be refused in its entirety;

(b) The Applicant be ordered to pay the costs of these opposition proceedings to the OPPONENT;

(c) Such other and further relief as the Registrar of Trade Marks may deem just and proper be granted to the OPPONENT.

(c) I am duly authorized to file this Notice of Opposition;

4. STATEMENT OF FACTS
That the OPPONENT, WAJAHAT RAMZAN, is engaged in the business of manufacturing, marketing, and trading of BEERS; MINERAL AND AERATED WATERS AND OTHER NON-ALCOHOLIC DRINKS; FRUIT DRINKS AND FRUIT JUICES; SYRUPS AND OTHER PREPARATIONS FOR MAKING BEVERAGES under the trade marks BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY.

That the OPPONENT is the original creator, earliest adopter, and prior user of the marks BIG PARTY (adopted in 2006) and PURE PULPY. The OPPONENT has been continuously and extensively using these marks throughout Pakistan long prior to the Applicant's filing date of 23 October 2025. In Pakistan, prior use has precedence over registration, and even unregistered trademark rights can be enforced through passing-off claims where distinctiveness has been established through continuous use. 

That the OPPONENT, in order to distinguish its goods from competitors, adopted and used the marks BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY to identify its superior quality products, which are marketed, sold, and distributed by the OPPONENT.

That to secure its intellectual property rights, the OPPONENT applied for registration of the trademark BIG PARTY under Application No. 515608 in Class 32 earliest in 2006 and has also obtained Copyright Registrations bearing Nos. 2021/4718 and 2023/1878 for its artistic works/labels, including the mark PURE PULPY. 

That since its inception in 2006, the OPPONENT's business has grown exceptionally and achieved a top ranking in the beverage industry through professionally trained workforce, commitment, and superior quality products, earning envious goodwill and recognition throughout Pakistan and beyond.

That the OPPONENT has made considerable investment in marketing, promotion, and advertising of its trademarks BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY, due to which it has acquired substantial market recognition, brand image, and customer association for goods being sold under these marks.

That the OPPONENT has immense presence throughout Pakistan due to rapid business expansion and has acquired unmatched goodwill and notoriety in the beverage sector by consistently maintaining exceptionally high standards of quality for its customers.

That the OPPONENT is also engaged in exports of its products bearing the said trademarks to various international markets, further establishing its reputation beyond the borders of Pakistan.

That it has come to the notice of the OPPONENT that the Applicant, SHEIKH KHALID BASHIR, Trading as AQSA FOODS, has applied for registration of the trademark "BIG PULPY" under Application No. 842408 in Class 32.

That the Applicant's mark "BIG PULPY" is a deceptive imitation and a copy of the OPPONENT's registered copyrighted mark "PURE PULPY" as it incorporates the distinctive and dominant element "PULPY" which is the key distinctive feature of the OPPONENT's mark.

That the Applicant's mark "BIG PULPY" is also visually, phonetically, and structurally deceptively similar to the OPPONENT's well-known mark "BIG PARTY" as both marks contain the word "BIG" as a prefix and are used for identical goods in Class 32, thereby creating confusion and deception in the course of trade.

That the Applicant's mark "BIG PULPY" is essentially a combination of the OPPONENT's two distinct brands "BIG PARTY" and "PURE PULPY" , which demonstrates the Applicant's intentional and mala fide attempt to ride upon the OPPONENT's goodwill, reputation, and market presence.

That the Applicant's mark "BIG PULPY" is also designed to mimic the get-up, trade dress, and overall commercial impression of both the OPPONENT's BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY brands, which further demonstrates the Applicant's intentional and mala fide attempt to ride upon the OPPONENT's goodwill.

That the goods covered by the Applicant's mark and those in respect of which the OPPONENT is using its trademarks BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY are identical (beverages in Class 32), which inevitably leads to confusion and deception among consumers and traders.

That the Applicant legally and factually cannot claim to be the owner or proprietor of the impugned trademark BIG PULPY, as by virtue of prior use, prior applications, and copyright registrations, the proprietorship in respect of the marks BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY vests exclusively and solely with the OPPONENT. Any claim by the Applicant to proprietorship of an identical/deceptively similar trademark is misconceived, invalid, and dishonest.

That the Applicant's application appears to have been adopted with bad intentions solely to benefit from the OPPONENT's goodwill, reputation, and market recognition. The Applicant only seeks to make easy profits at the expense, reputation, and goodwill of the OPPONENT by misleading consumers into believing that its goods originate from the OPPONENT.

That the OPPONENT is not aware of any legitimate use being made by the Applicant of the mark BIG PULPY, and any claim which the Applicant has made or intends to make is illegal, malicious, and dishonest.

That the Applicant has adopted and applied for registration of the trademark BIG PULPY in bad faith, with complete knowledge of the OPPONENT's prior rights, trademarks, and copyrights, with the ulterior motive to encroach upon the reputation and goodwill of the OPPONENT's well-known and prior marks BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY. 

That the Applicant has also filed another application No. 735718 for "FINE PULPY" , which targets the OPPONENT's PURE PULPY brand, demonstrating a clear pattern of mala fide conduct and a deliberate strategy to dilute and infringe the OPPONENT's intellectual property rights.

5. GROUNDS OF OPPOSITION
GROUND 1: Prior Use, Ownership, and Breach of Copyright (Sections 14, 17(4)(a), 17(4)(b), 18(1)(c) & 29 of the Trade Marks Ordinance, 2001 read with Section 10 of the Copyright Ordinance, 1962)
The OPPONENT is the earliest adopter, prior user, and original creator of the marks BIG PARTY (since 2006) and PURE PULPY. The OPPONENT holds Copyright Registrations bearing Nos. 2021/4718 and 2023/1878 for the artistic works/labels incorporating the PURE PULPY mark.  The Applicant's mark BIG PULPY is a substantial reproduction and deceptive imitation of the OPPONENT's copyrighted artistic work as it incorporates the dominant word "PULPY" . Registration would constitute a breach of copyright—a ground specifically recognized under Section 29 for opposing registration.  Furthermore, the Applicant's mark BIG PULPY is structurally similar to BIG PARTY (sharing the prefix "BIG" ) and identical in goods. Under Section 17(4)(a) , earlier unregistered rights are preserved; under Section 17(4)(b) , earlier trademark applications are a bar to registration; and under Section 18(1)(c) , the OPPONENT is the earlier proprietor. Furthermore, the OPPONENT's rights are protected under common law through passing-off, as unregistered trademark rights can be enforced where distinctiveness has been acquired through continuous use.  The Applicant cannot claim ownership, and the application is liable to be refused under Section 14 (absolute grounds) as the mark is devoid of any distinctive character, with the dominant elements "BIG" and "PULPY" merely copied from the OPPONENT's marks.

GROUND 2: Passing Off, Misappropriation of Goodwill, and Deceptive Similarity (Sections 17(4)(a), 29(4)(a), 29(4)(b), 29(3)(a), 29(3)(c), 67(1)(a) & (g) of the Trade Marks Ordinance, 2001)
The OPPONENT has acquired substantial goodwill and reputation through continuous use since 2006, nationwide sales, exports, and high-quality products under the marks BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY.  The Applicant's mark BIG PULPY is identical in goods and deceptively similar in appearance, phonetics, and structure to both of the OPPONENT's marks. BIG PULPY copies the "BIG" prefix from BIG PARTY and the "PULPY" element from PURE PULPY, combining both to create a mark that is confusingly similar to both of the OPPONENT's brands. This constitutes a clear misrepresentation likely to deceive the public and cause confusion, amounting to passing off. Pakistan recognizes common law trademark rights based on prior use, enforceable through passing off.  The Applicant's action constitutes unfair and unethical business practices under Section 67(1)(a) & (g) of the Ordinance, as the Applicant seeks to pass off its low-quality products as originating from the OPPONENT or its authorized licensees, causing great loss to the OPPONENT's business and reputation. Furthermore, under Section 29, opposition can be based on grounds including lack of distinctiveness and filing in bad faith. 

GROUND 3: Bad Faith, Lack of Distinctiveness, and Dilution (Sections 14(1), 18(1)(c), 29(3)(a), 29(3)(c), 86 & 18(1)(c) of the Trade Marks Ordinance, 2001 read with Paris Convention)
The Applicant's adoption of the mark BIG PULPY is a calculated and dishonest attempt to combine the OPPONENT's two distinctive brands BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY to ride upon the OPPONENT's enormous goodwill. The Applicant filed the application with full knowledge of the OPPONENT's prior rights, as evidenced by the deceptive combination of the OPPONENT's marks. The filing of two separate applications (FINE PULPY and BIG PULPY) targeting both of the OPPONENT's brands demonstrates a clear pattern of mala fide conduct and a systematic attempt to dilute and infringe the OPPONENT's intellectual property rights. The impugned mark is devoid of any distinctive character under Section 14(1), as it merely copies the OPPONENT's marks. The Applicant's actions smack of mala fide and dishonesty under Section 29(3)(a) & (c). The OPPONENT's marks BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY are liable to be protected as well-known marks under Section 86 of the Ordinance read with the Paris Convention, being earlier marks defined under Section 18(1)(c).  Registration of the impugned mark would cause dilution, deception, and confusion, and the application is therefore liable to be refused.

GROUND 4: Pattern of Mala Fide Conduct – Multiple Applications Targeting Opponent's Brands (Sections 29(3)(a) & 29(3)(c) of the Trade Marks Ordinance, 2001)
The Applicant has filed two separate applications for marks that are deceptively similar to the OPPONENT's two distinct brands—FINE PULPY (Application No. 735718) targeting PURE PULPY and BIG PULPY (Application No. 842408) targeting both BIG PARTY and PURE PULPY. This demonstrates a clear and deliberate pattern of mala fide conduct and a systematic attempt to dilute, infringe, and misappropriate the OPPONENT's intellectual property rights. The Applicants are clearly aware of the OPPONENT's brands and have intentionally filed multiple applications to cover all variations, which is evidence of bad faith and dishonest intention under Section 29(3)(a) & (c) of the Ordinance. 

6. PRAYER / REQUEST
In view of the foregoing facts and grounds, the OPPONENT respectfully prays that:

(a) The Application No. 842408 for registration of the trade mark "BIG PULPY" in Class 32 be refused in its entirety;

(b) The Applicant be ordered to pay the costs of these opposition proceedings to the OPPONENT;

(c) Such other and further relief as the Registrar of Trade Marks may deem just and proper be granted to the OPPONENT.

7/27/26, 6:02 PM - Brandex: 1. RENT AGREEMENT
RENT AGREEMENT

This Rent Agreement is made and executed on this 01st day of June 2026, between:

Mr. Ehtasham Ud Din Siddiqui, S/o Shafique ud din Siddiqui, resident of Junejo Colony, Tralai Kalan, Islamabad, CNIC No. 37405-1127327-5, hereinafter referred to as the "Landlord/Owner" (which expression shall where the context so permits include his heirs, successors, legal representatives and assigns);

AND

Mr. Shahzad Hussain, S/o Sahzada, resident of House No. K-3, Sohan Colony, Muslim Town, Rawalpindi, CNIC No. 37405-0291195-1, hereinafter referred to as the "Tenant" (which expression shall where the context so permits include his heirs, successors, legal representatives and assigns).

The Landlord hereby lets out to the Tenant the residential premises situated at:

House No. N/1-8, Sohan Colony, Muslim Town, Rawalpindi, measuring approximately 13 × 38 feet.

Terms and Conditions

The monthly rent shall be PKR 20,000/- (Rupees Twenty Thousand Only) , payable in advance on or before the 10th day of each calendar month.

The tenancy shall commence from 10-06-2026 and shall continue on a monthly basis unless terminated by either party giving one month's written notice to the other.

The Tenant shall use the premises for lawful residential purposes only.

The Tenant shall pay electricity, gas, water and other utility bills relating to his consumption during the tenancy.

The Tenant shall not sublet or transfer possession of the premises without the written consent of the Landlord.

The Tenant shall pay a security deposit of PKR 20,000/- (Rupees Twenty Thousand Only) to the Landlord at the time of signing this Agreement, which shall be refundable at the time of vacating the premises, subject to the deduction of any outstanding dues or damages.

The Landlord shall be responsible for the structural maintenance of the premises, while the Tenant shall be responsible for day-to-day maintenance.

The Landlord hereby authorizes the Tenant to apply for and obtain a new electricity connection/meter from IESCO (Islamabad Electric Supply Company) in the Tenant's own name for the above-mentioned premises. The Landlord shall provide all necessary documents, including but not limited to ownership documents and CNIC copy, to facilitate such application.

Both parties agree to comply with the terms of this Agreement.

Subject: Urgent Request for Cancellation/Withdrawal of Company Easy Exit Scheme (CEES) Application filed for FARS METALS (PRIVATE) LIMITED – CUIN: 0240129

Dear Sir/Madam,

We are writing to bring to your kind attention a grave clerical error committed by our office in the submission of the Company Easy Exit Scheme (CEES) application bearing Application ID: 20260701_44783693.

Due to the striking similarity in the names of our two associated companies, we inadvertently submitted the seasing/striking-off application for FARS METALS (PRIVATE) LIMITED (CUIN: 0240129) instead of our own other  company, FARS INTERNATIONAL (SMC-PRIVATE) LIMITED (CUIN: 0111131).

The Notice under Section 426(2) of the Companies Act, 2017 has been received by FARS METALS (PRIVATE) LIMITED on 14th July, 2026 in respect of this erroneous application.

We hereby request your good office to kindly cancel/withdraw the CEES application (Application ID: 20260701_44783693) submitted for FARS METALS (PRIVATE) LIMITED at the earliest. The said company is active and operational and does not intend to pursue the seasing process. The submission was made purely due to a clerical oversight on account of the similar corporate names.

An early cancellation of this application is of utmost importance to save the company from significant financial and operational losses, as well as unnecessary regulatory complications.

We sincerely regret the inconvenience caused and assure you of our utmost care in all future filings. We shall be grateful for your prompt action in this matter.

Enclosed are copies of the following documents for your reference:

1. Certificate of Incorporation of FARS INTERNATIONAL (SMC-PRIVATE) LIMITED (CUIN: 0111131)
2. CEES Notice received for FARS METALS (PRIVATE) LIMITED (Application ID: 20260701_44783693)

Thank you for your kind consideration.

Yours faithfully,

For and on behalf of

Date: _______________

To:
The Registrar
Business Hub Islamabad
Securities and Exchange Commission of Pakistan (SECP)
Mezzanine Floor, NIC Building, 63 Jinnah Avenue, Blue Area, Islamabad

Subject: Urgent Request for Cancellation/Withdrawal of Company Easy Exit Scheme (CEES) Application filed for FARS METALS (PRIVATE) LIMITED – CUIN: 0240129

Dear Sir/Madam,

We are writing to bring to your kind attention a grave clerical error committed by our office in the submission of the Company Easy Exit Scheme (CEES) application bearing Application ID: 20260701_44783693.

Due to the striking similarity in the names of our two associated companies, we inadvertently submitted the seasing/striking-off application for FARS METALS (PRIVATE) LIMITED (CUIN: 0240129) instead of our own company, FARS INTERNATIONAL (SMC-PRIVATE) LIMITED (CUIN: 0111131).

The Notice under Section 426(2) of the Companies Act, 2017 has been received by FARS METALS (PRIVATE) LIMITED on 14th July, 2026 in respect of this erroneous application.

We hereby request your good office to kindly cancel/withdraw the CEES application (Application ID: 20260701_44783693) submitted for FARS METALS (PRIVATE) LIMITED at the earliest. The said company is active and operational and does not intend to pursue the seasing process. The submission was made purely due to a clerical oversight on account of the similar corporate names.

An early cancellation of this application is of utmost importance to save the company from significant financial and operational losses, as well as unnecessary regulatory complications.

We sincerely regret the inconvenience caused and assure you of our utmost care in all future filings. We shall be grateful for your prompt action in this matter.

Enclosed are copies of the following documents for your reference:

1. Certificate of Incorporation of FARS INTERNATIONAL (SMC-PRIVATE) LIMITED (CUIN: 0111131)
2. CEES Notice received for FARS METALS (PRIVATE) LIMITED (Application ID: 20260701_44783693)

Thank you for your kind consideration.

Yours faithfully,

For and on behalf of
FARS INTERNATIONAL (SMC-PRIVATE) LIMITED

8/13/26, 9:05 PM - ᥫ᭡፝֟፝  ❍ᵁイ亗⎳٨ꪝ ༗: Yes. I see **several substantive mistakes**, not just grammar/formatting issues. The biggest concern is that some paragraphs make legal assertions that are internally inconsistent with the facts stated elsewhere.

## 1. Critical factual inconsistencies

### A. Application title is inconsistent

At the beginning:

> Application No. 842062 ... titled **GOLO MOLO**

Later:

> “THE SUBJECT APPLICATION NO. 842062 IN CLASS 30 TITLED **GOOL MOOL** MAY KINDLY BE REFUSED.”

This should be **GOLO MOLO** if 842062 is the applicant's mark.

---

### B. Opponent's mark is inconsistently spelled

You use:

- **GOOL MOOL**
- **GOLMOL**
- **GOOL MOOL**
- **GOLO MOLO**

Paragraph 5 says:

> “applied for registration of the trademark and copyright **GOLMOL**”

But the opponent's claimed mark throughout is **GOOL MOOL**, and the table says:

> GOOL MOOL C30 — 873579 — REGISTERED

If **873579 is actually registered**, then paragraph 5 is particularly problematic because it calls it a **pending application**.

---

### C. Registration vs. pending status contradiction

Paragraph 5:

> “THE OPPONENT HAS ALSO ALREADY APPLIED FOR REGISTRATION...”

The table later states:

> **GOOL MOOL C30 — 873579 — REGISTERED**

And paragraph 11 says:

> “PENDING TRADEMARK AND COPYRIGHT APPLICATIONS”

If 873579 is registered, don't describe the same mark/application as merely pending.

You need to distinguish precisely between:

- registered trademark;
- registered copyright;
- pending trademark application;
- pending copyright application.

This is one of the most important corrections.

---

## 2. Paragraph 1 has a major grammatical/structural error

This portion:

> “THE OPPONENT’S BUSINESS IS KNOWN FOR ITS DISTINCTIVE BRANDS...”

is preceded by:

> “SPICES; ICE THE OPPONENT HAS BUILT...”

There is clearly missing punctuation/full stop after **ICE**.

Also:

> “FIELD. OF COFFEE”

should be:

> “FIELD OF COFFEE”

And:

> “TRADE DRESS..”

has double punctuation.

---

## 3. Services/goods classification issue

The applicant's specification says:

> “RETAILING AND WHOLESALING OF SERVICES FEATURING SNACKS...”

This wording itself should be checked carefully against the **actual specification appearing in Application No. 842062 / Journal No. 904**.

There is a potentially important distinction between:

- goods in Class 30; and
- retail/wholesale services, which may fall under Class 35.

You shouldn't casually characterize everything as "goods/services" without matching the actual application specification.

### Recommendation

Use the **exact wording published by IPO** for Application 842062 rather than reconstructing it.

---

## 4. Paragraph 2 makes a copyright claim that needs factual support

You state:

> “THE OPPONENT IS THE ORIGINAL ADOPTER, CREATOR, AUTHOR, MANUFACTURER AND PROPRIETOR OF THE TRADEMARK AND ORIGINAL ARTISTIC WORK...”

A trademark and an artistic work are legally distinct subject matters.

Also, **manufacturer** isn't necessarily synonymous with creator/author of artwork.

Better to establish separately:

1. adoption/proprietorship of the trademark;
2. ownership of the artistic work;
3. copyright registration, if applicable;
4. use of the artwork/packaging/trade dress.

This becomes especially important because your table identifies specific copyright registrations.

---

## 5. Paragraph 3 needs evidence of use since 2010

You state:

> “CONTINUOUSLY... USED... SINCE AT LEAST 2010.”

That's a strong factual assertion.

If you have evidence from 2010 onward, good. Otherwise, it is risky to put **2010** in the pleading without documentary support.

Useful supporting evidence could include:

- old invoices;
- packaging;
- advertisements;
- distributor records;
- sales records;
- photographs;
- tax records;
- social-media material;
- dated catalogues;
- purchase orders;
- website records.

---

## 6. Paragraph 4 contains a potentially misleading statement

You say:

> “THE OPPONENT IS THE REGISTERED PROPRIETOR OF A FAMILY OF TRADEMARKS AND COPYRIGHTS WITH THE INTELLECTUAL PROPERTY ORGANIZATION OF PAKISTAN (IPO)...”

Then list:

> SMACK COCOA  
> ZARAR NICE  
> ZARAR ALBINA  
> SHINTO  
> ANAS FOOD  
> ZARAR FOOD

But your later table is specifically labelled:

> **COPYRIGHT REGISTRATIONS**

You need to distinguish **trademarks from copyrights**.

Don't call something a "registered trademark" unless the underlying IPO record actually shows it as a trademark registration.

---

## 7. Paragraph 6 has weak/possibly incorrect analysis of the marks

You write:

> “THE DOMINANT, ESSENTIAL AND DISTINCTIVE FEATURES... ESPECIALLY THE LEADING ELEMENT ‘GOL’... HAVE BEEN COPIED.”

But:

**GOOL MOOL**  
vs.  
**GOLO MOLO**

The similarity argument can be made, but saying that the **leading element "GOL" has been copied** is not particularly precise because the marks aren't simply identical after removal of vowels.

A stronger pleading should compare:

- visual similarity;
- phonetic similarity;
- syllabic structure;
- overall cadence/rhythm;
- sequence of consonants/vowels;
- conceptual impression, if applicable;
- similarity of goods/trade channels;
- imperfect recollection.

Avoid overclaiming that every element has been copied.

---

## 8. "In the opponent's own words" should be removed

Paragraph 6 contains:

> “IN THE OPPONENT’S OWN WORDS: THE APPLICANT IS MAKING A COPY...”

This is strange because **the opponent is the party filing the opposition**. You're essentially quoting your own pleading inside your own pleading.

Remove that sentence and state the allegation directly.

---

## 9. Paragraph 7 mixes goods and services too loosely

> “THE GOODS/SERVICES FOR WHICH THE IMPUGNED MARK HAS BEEN APPLIED...”

Again, use the **exact legal specification**.

If the application covers particular goods and/or Class 35 services, identify them accurately.

---

## 10. Paragraph 8: "passing off" needs better treatment

You state:

> “REGISTRATION AND USE... WOULD CONSTITUTE PASSING OFF”

Passing off is not simply another synonym for trademark similarity.

The pleading should identify the classic elements:

1. goodwill/reputation;
2. misrepresentation;
3. likelihood of damage.

You already have the ingredients, but the legal reasoning should be more disciplined.

---

## 11. Paragraph 9 makes a very serious allegation

You say:

> “THE APPLICANT HAS ADOPTED... WITH FULL KNOWLEDGE...”

and:

> “THE ADOPTION CANNOT BE COINCIDENTAL.”

This is a **bad-faith/dishonest adoption allegation**.

Don't make this stronger than your evidence permits.

If you have evidence that the applicant knew of the opponent's mark, state the factual basis—for example:

- prior dealings;
- same market;
- same distributors;
- prior correspondence;
- prior knowledge;
- copying of artwork/packaging;
- geographic/business relationship.

Otherwise the allegation may be vulnerable.

---

# 12. Paragraph 10 needs statutory-section verification

You say:

> “REGISTRATION... WOULD BE CONTRARY TO SECTIONS 17, 18, 19...”

Then paragraph 12 adds:

> “SECTIONS 14, 17, 18, 19 AND 22”

And paragraph 16 adds:

> “SECTIONS 14, 17, 22, AND 39”

These sections should **not be inserted merely because they sound relevant**.

For a legal pleading, I would verify the **current text of the Trade Marks Ordinance, 2001 and applicable Rules** and map each ground to the actual statutory provision.

This is particularly important because the document repeatedly uses different sections for:

- distinctiveness;
- earlier marks;
- bad faith;
- opposition;
- refusal.

---

## 13. Paragraph 12 says "not capable of distinguishing" but your argument is actually different

You say:

> “THE IMPUGNED MARK IS NOT CAPABLE OF DISTINGUISHING...”

But your primary argument appears to be:

> **deceptive similarity with an earlier mark + likelihood of confusion + prior rights + bad faith**

Those are conceptually different grounds from saying the mark is inherently incapable of distinguishing.

Don't unnecessarily introduce a distinctiveness ground unless it genuinely applies.

---

# 14. Paragraph 14 is logically weak

You state:

> “THE OPPONENT HAS ESTABLISHED A FAMILY/SERIES OF MARKS USING THE PREFIX ‘ZARAR’ AND OTHER DISTINCTIVE ELEMENTS, INCLUDING THE MARK ‘GOOL MOOL’.”

But **GOOL MOOL does not use the ZARAR prefix**.

Then:

> “THE IMPUGNED MARK IS DECEPTIVELY SIMILAR TO THE OPPONENT’S FAMILY...”

This is difficult to follow.

The **GOOL MOOL** similarity case should be based primarily on **GOOL MOOL itself**, not an alleged ZARAR family unless you have a legally relevant reason for bringing the ZARAR portfolio into the analysis.

I would substantially revise or potentially delete paragraph 14.

---

# 15. Paragraph 15 has a potentially serious date issue

You state:

> “THIS OPPOSITION IS FILED WITHIN THE STATUTORY PERIOD...”

and:

> “JOURNAL NO. 904 DATED 25 JUNE 2026.”

But your document is dated:

> **13 AUGUST 2026**

You need to calculate the actual opposition deadline under the applicable law/rules based on the **publication date and statutory opposition period**.

Don't simply say it is within time without confirming the calculation.

This should be verified before filing.

---

# 16. Paragraph 15 says "statutory period" but doesn't state the deadline

For a formal opposition, I'd prefer something like:

> "The present opposition is being filed within the prescribed period calculated from the date of publication..."

**only after confirming the actual period.**

If an extension is involved, that also needs to be stated accurately.

---

# 17. The copyright table has a factual formatting problem

Your row:

> 19. ZARRAR DREAM CARAMEL C30 (PENDING) | [blank] | PENDING

If it's pending and **there is no registration number**, calling the column "REG NO" is misleading.

Better table structure:

| S. No. | Title | Registration/Application No. | Status |
|---|---|---|---|

Then enter the relevant application number for the pending matter.

---

# 18. "GOOL MOOL C30 — 873579 — REGISTERED" is extremely important

If **873579 is registered**, then the entire opposition becomes stronger than the current draft suggests.

You currently say:

> “based on our applied trademark application no. 873579”

But if it's registered, you should consistently refer to it as the **registered trademark** and give its registration status accurately.

That is a significant drafting upgrade.

---

# 19. Paragraph 16 duplicates earlier grounds

Paragraphs 6, 9, 10, 12 and 16 all essentially repeat:

- imitation;
- similarity;
- bad faith;
- confusion;
- statutory violation.

This makes the opposition unnecessarily repetitive.

A stronger pleading would organize the grounds into:

1. Opponent's ownership/prior rights;
2. Prior use and goodwill;
3. Similarity of marks;
4. Similarity of goods/services;
5. Likelihood of confusion;
6. Bad faith;
7. Copyright/artistic-work copying;
8. Statutory grounds;
9. Damage/prejudice.

---

# 20. Paragraph 17 is not ideally worded

> “THE APPLICANT CANNOT RIGHTFULLY CLAIM PROPRIETORSHIP OVER THE MARK IN COURT”

This is awkward.

The issue before the Tribunal is registration/proprietorship under the applicable trademark law—not whether the applicant can "claim proprietorship in court."

Also:

> “THIS OPPOSITION MUST BE ALLOWED”

Better legal pleading language is generally:

> “the opposition may kindly be allowed”

or equivalent formal wording.

---

# 21. Prayer contains a major grammatical mistake

You write:

> “APPLICANT OF TM-5 BEING TRUE AND GENUINE OWNER...”

This should be **Opponent**, not Applicant.

You are the **Opponent** in TM-5.

This is a critical error.

---

# 22. Prayer again uses wrong mark

You write:

> “APPLICATION NO. 842062... TITLED GOOL MOOL”

But the entire case identifies the opposed mark as:

> **GOLO MOLO**

Correct this.

---

# 23. "THE SUBJECT TRADEMARK" is ambiguous

In:

> “THE OPPONENT IS THE ORIGINAL ADOPTER AND PROPRIETOR OF THE SUBJECT TRADEMARK...”

There are two marks in the proceedings.

Use:

> **“the Opponent's trademark ‘GOOL MOOL’”**

instead of "subject trademark."

---

# 24. Address formatting

You have:

> “DROP AT ABDULLAH CENTRE”

This looks like it may be a typo.

If the intended wording is **“Shop/Office No. …”**, **“Drop at”**, or some particular address terminology, verify the actual address.

Also check:

> “TARLAI”

and the PTCL Exchange reference against your official address-for-service records.

For a legal filing, the address should be **exactly the address you want IPO to use for service**.

---

# 25. "TO THE REGISTRAR..." is fine, but jurisdiction/location should be checked

You have:

> “THE REGISTRAR OF TRADE MARKS, IPO, LAHORE/TMR KARACHI.”

Make sure the filing is being directed to the correct office/registry for the proceeding.

Don't leave an ambiguous slash formulation if the official form requires a particular registry.

---

# 26. "OUR ADDRESS" vs. formal applicant/opponent wording

You use:

> “OUR ADDRESS FOR SERVICE...”

For a formal TM-5, check the prescribed form language and use the terminology required by the applicable **Trade Marks Rules, 2004**.

---

# 27. "BY ITS ADVOCATE" is grammatically questionable

The opponent is:

> **MUHAMMAD QASIM SHAFIQ**

an individual/proprietor.

Then:

> “BY ITS ADVOCATE”

For an individual, **“his Advocate”** or the prescribed form language would normally be more appropriate than "its."

However, use the exact wording required by the prescribed form if there is one.

---

# 28. Signature section needs cleaner separation

You have:

> MUHAMMAD QASIM SHAFIQ  
> (PROPRIETOR)  
> BY ITS ADVOCATE  
> MUMTAZ ALI GHAURI  
> (ADVOCATE HIGH COURT)

This should be formatted so it is absolutely clear:

- who is the Opponent;
- who signs for the Opponent;
- who is the authorized advocate;
- whether a power of attorney/authority is on record.

---

# 29. Typographical errors

I found several:

### Paragraph 1
> “FIELD. OF”

→ **FIELD OF**

### Paragraph 6
> “HE MARK”

→ **THE MARK**

### Paragraph 8
> “PUBLIC. CONSUMERS”

Could be a paragraph break, but punctuation/formatting should be standardized.

### Paragraph 13
> “MARKET THE OPPONENT”

Needs punctuation:

> “MARKET. THE OPPONENT...”

### Paragraph 15
> “TRADE MARKS JOURNAL NO. 904 DATED 25 JUNE 2026”

Fine if verified.

### Paragraph 16
> “SQUARELY VIOLATE”

This is stylistically aggressive; **“contravene”** or **“fall within the prohibition of”** may be more appropriate depending on the provision.

---

# 30. "Well-known" claim needs evidence

You repeatedly say:

> “well-known”

and:

> “well-known mark”

This is a significant legal characterization.

If you intend to rely on well-known-mark protection, I would attach/support it with evidence such as:

- duration of use;
- geographical extent;
- sales turnover;
- advertising expenditure;
- market share;
- distribution network;
- consumer recognition;
- registrations;
- enforcement history;
- awards/media recognition where relevant.

Otherwise, the safer formulation is to establish **substantial goodwill and reputation** first and plead well-known status only where evidence supports it.

---

# 31. "Internationally" should not be included without evidence

Paragraph 1:

> “IN PAKISTAN AND/OR INTERNATIONALLY”

This is unnecessarily broad.

If the opponent has international use, identify the relevant jurisdictions/evidence. Otherwise simply plead Pakistan.

---

# 32. "Exclusive proprietary rights" needs careful wording

You say:

> “acquired exclusive proprietary rights, common law rights, goodwill and reputation”

Prior use can support goodwill and passing-off rights, but **"exclusive proprietary rights"** should be used carefully, particularly if there are registration issues.

Since you now state **GOOL MOOL 873579 is registered**, the registration itself should carry much of this argument.

---

# 33. "No honest concurrent use" needs factual foundation

Paragraph 11:

> “THE APPLICANT HAS ... NO HONEST CONCURRENT USE”

This is a conclusion.

If the applicant hasn't demonstrated concurrent use, you can plead that **no facts have been disclosed establishing honest concurrent use**, rather than simply asserting the conclusion.

---

# 34. The biggest strategic issue: your opponent's rights should be presented chronologically

Your strongest story appears to be:

**GOOL MOOL → use since 2010 → goodwill → artwork/copyright → IPO filings/registration → applicant's later GOLO MOLO application → similarity → same/related goods → confusion → bad faith.**

But the current document jumps between:

- trademarks;
- copyrights;
- ZARAR;
- GOLMOL;
- well-known status;
- passing off;
- statutory sections.

A chronological and evidence-driven structure would be much stronger.

---

## Most urgent corrections before filing

I would **not file this version yet**. At minimum, verify/fix these 10 points:

| Priority | Issue | Action |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | 842062 called GOOL MOOL in prayer | Change to **GOLO MOLO** |
| **2** | 873579 called pending/applied | Verify status; table says **REGISTERED** |
| **3** | GOOL MOOL vs GOLMOL | Standardize correct opponent mark |
| **4** | Applicant/Opponent mix-up | Prayer says **Applicant** instead of **Opponent** |
| **5** | Goods/services wording | Match IPO Journal exactly |
| **6** | Statutory sections | Verify every cited section against current law |
| **7** | Opposition deadline | Calculate from **25 June 2026** |
| **8** | Copyright vs trademark registrations | Separate them accurately |
| **9** | ZARAR family argument | Rework/remove unless legally relevant |
| **10** | Bad-faith allegation | Support with actual facts/evidence |

### One particularly important point

The document currently says both:

> **GOOL MOOL — 873579 — REGISTERED**

and

> **“PENDING TRADEMARK AND COPYRIGHT APPLICATIONS”**

Those cannot simply coexist without clarification. **Before anything else, verify what IPO record 873579 actually represents and its current status.**

If you give me the **IPO Journal page/entry for Application 842062 and the IPO record/status for 873579**, I can check the opposition against those exact particulars and identify the remaining legal/factual inconsistencies.

8/17/26, 8:35 PM - Brandex: ❖════════════=====================═════════════❖
8/17/26, 8:40 PM - Brandex: COUNTER-STATEMENT

The Applicant categorically denies each and every allegation, assertion and contention contained in the Notice of Opposition except those expressly admitted herein.

The Applicant is the original and bona fide adopter, creator and proprietor of the trademark HURAIN, which was independently adopted and used by the Applicant since 2015. The Applicant subsequently applied for registration of HURAIN under Application No. 747260 dated 21.03.2024.

The Applicant’s adoption and use of HURAIN was neither dishonest nor subsequent to the Opponent’s claimed rights. The Applicant shall rely upon documentary evidence, including old packaging, labels, invoices, sales records, photographs, promotional material and other available evidence demonstrating use of HURAIN since 2015.

REPLY TO PARA 1:

The contents of paragraph 1 are denied to the extent that they are intended to confer any proprietary right upon the Opponent in the trademark HURAIN or any exclusive right over the field of cosmetics merely by virtue of carrying on business under the trading name BABA COSMETICS since 2012. Mere commencement of cosmetic business does not establish proprietary rights in the disputed trademark HURAIN.

REPLY TO PARA 2:

The contents are denied for want of strict proof. The alleged status as a registered taxpayer, existence of a business and alleged reputation do not establish ownership of the trademark HURAIN. The Applicant’s adoption and use since 2015 predates the Opponent’s claimed adoption of HORAIN COSMETICS in 2019.

REPLY TO PARA 3:

The contents are denied insofar as they are relevant to the present opposition. The Opponent’s alleged trademarks, including HORAIN, SAY SHINE, SIKABLE, BABA COSMETICS, BABA APPLE and others, are separate marks and have no bearing upon the Applicant’s independent proprietorship of HURAIN. The Opponent is put to strict proof regarding each alleged trademark, its date of adoption, first use, registration status and actual commercial use.

REPLY TO PARA 4:

The contents are specifically denied insofar as they allege prior rights capable of defeating the Applicant’s application. The Opponent itself states that it adopted and used HORAIN COSMETICS since 2019 and filed its trademark application on 24.12.2019. The Applicant adopted and continuously used HURAIN since 2015, approximately four years before the Opponent’s claimed adoption of HORAIN COSMETICS. Therefore, the Opponent cannot legitimately describe the Applicant as a subsequent adopter or infringer of a mark which, according to the Opponent’s own pleading, was adopted by the Opponent later.

REPLY TO PARA 5:

The contents are denied. The alleged promotion, reputation, quality and goodwill of HORAIN COSMETICS do not establish any proprietary interest in the Applicant’s independently adopted mark HURAIN. The Opponent is required to establish the alleged extent, geographical scope, turnover, advertising expenditure and actual consumer recognition of HORAIN COSMETICS by cogent documentary evidence.

REPLY TO PARA 6:

The contents are admitted only to the extent that the Applicant has filed Application No. 747260 in Class 03 for HURAIN. The remaining allegations are denied. The Applicant has committed no infringement or wrongdoing. The application was filed to secure statutory protection for the Applicant’s own trademark, which had already been independently adopted and used since 2015.

REPLY TO PARA 7:

The contents are emphatically denied. The allegation that the Applicant dishonestly adopted an almost identical trademark is false and unsupported by the actual chronology. The Applicant adopted HURAIN in 2015, whereas the Opponent claims adoption of HORAIN COSMETICS only in 2019. Consequently, the Applicant could not have copied or dishonestly appropriated a mark which, according to the Opponent’s own case, had not yet been adopted by the Opponent. The Applicant is the bona fide adopter and user of his own trademark.

REPLY TO PARA 8:

The contents are categorically denied. There was no dishonest intention, wrongful gain or intention to create any false association with the Opponent. The Applicant’s adoption of HURAIN was independent and bona fide and dates back to 2015. The allegation of bad faith is therefore unsupported and contradicted by the chronology pleaded by the Opponent itself.

REPLY TO PARA 9:

The contents are denied. The Opponent’s alleged proprietorship of HORAIN COSMETICS cannot automatically extend to the independently adopted trademark HURAIN. The Applicant has been using HURAIN since 2015 and has established his own commercial identity under the said mark. The allegation of passing off is misconceived and denied.

REPLY TO PARA 10:

The contents are denied. The Applicant specifically denies that HURAIN is deceptively similar to the Opponent’s overall trademark HORAIN COSMETICS in the manner alleged. The marks must be considered as a whole, taking into consideration their overall appearance, presentation, logo, stylization, get-up and commercial context. The Applicant’s trademark has its own independently created presentation and distinctive commercial identity. Moreover, the Applicant’s adoption and use since 2015 predates the Opponent’s claimed adoption in 2019.

REPLY TO PARA 11:

The contents are denied. The Applicant has not made any unauthorized use of the Opponent’s trademark. On the contrary, the Applicant has independently developed his own goodwill and commercial identity under HURAIN since 2015. Any alleged loss or damage must be strictly proved and cannot be presumed merely from the existence of the Applicant’s application.

REPLY TO PARA 12:

The contents are denied. There is no reasonable basis for alleging that purchasers would believe that the Applicant’s HURAIN products are sponsored, licensed, endorsed or connected with the Opponent. The Applicant’s independent and longstanding use since 2015 is inconsistent with the allegation that HURAIN was subsequently adopted to create an association with HORAIN.

REPLY TO PARA 13:

The contents are denied. The Applicant denies that use of HURAIN would tarnish or damage the Opponent’s alleged reputation. The Opponent cannot claim damage merely by making a speculative assertion of reputation. Actual reputation and the alleged likelihood of damage must be established by reliable evidence.

REPLY TO PARA 14:

The contents are specifically denied. The allegation that the Applicant merely replaced the letter “O” with “U” is an artificial and legally insufficient method of comparing trademarks. The Applicant did not take the Opponent’s mark and replace a letter in it. The Applicant independently adopted HURAIN in 2015. The Opponent’s own pleading states that its claimed adoption of HORAIN was in 2019. Thus, the allegation of copying is chronologically impossible on the Opponent’s own pleaded facts.

REPLY TO PARA 15:

The contents are emphatically denied. The Applicant has legitimately used HURAIN in Pakistan since 2015. The allegation that the Applicant has never legitimately used the Impugned Trademark is false and is specifically put to strict proof. The Applicant shall produce documentary evidence demonstrating his longstanding use.

REPLY TO PARA 16:

The contents are denied. The Applicant has not attempted to lay hands upon the Opponent’s trademark. Rather, the Applicant’s use of HURAIN predates the Opponent’s claimed adoption of HORAIN. The Applicant has not adopted the mark with the intention of exploiting any alleged goodwill of the Opponent.

REPLY TO PARA 17:

The contents are categorically denied. The allegation that the Applicant adopted HURAIN after realizing the value of HORAIN is contrary to the admitted chronology. The Applicant adopted HURAIN in 2015, whereas the Opponent claims adoption of HORAIN in 2019. Accordingly, the Applicant could not have adopted HURAIN with knowledge of or intention to capitalize upon a trademark which the Opponent claims to have adopted four years later.

REPLY TO PARA 18:

The contents are denied. The allegation that the Applicant had numerous alternatives and deliberately selected HURAIN to obtain wrongful profits is speculative, unsupported and irrelevant. The Applicant adopted his mark independently and bona fide in 2015 and has since used it as his own commercial identifier.

REPLY TO PARA 19:

The contents are denied. The Applicant’s use of HURAIN is genuine and bona fide. The Applicant shall rely upon documentary evidence to establish the date, continuity and nature of his use. The Opponent cannot simply deny the Applicant’s prior use without producing evidence capable of rebutting the same.

REPLY TO PARA 20:

The contents are denied. The Applicant’s trademark does not fall within the alleged prohibition merely because the Opponent asserts similarity. The Applicant specifically denies that HURAIN was adopted in bad faith or that it is likely to deceive or confuse the purchasing public.

REPLY TO PARA 21:

The contents are denied. The allegation that HURAIN is devoid of distinctive character is misconceived. The Applicant’s mark has been independently adopted and used since 2015 and has acquired its own commercial identity. The Opponent cannot simultaneously claim exclusive distinctiveness in HORAIN and disregard the Applicant’s prior independent use of HURAIN.

REPLY TO PARA 22:

The contents are denied. The Applicant does not dispute the Registrar’s statutory duty to examine the application in accordance with law. However, the existence of an earlier registration does not automatically establish infringement or refusal where the Applicant has independent prior adoption and use and the statutory requirements for refusal are otherwise not established. The Opponent is put to strict proof of the precise scope, validity, subsistence, ownership and actual use of the alleged earlier mark.

REPLY TO PARA 23:

The contents are denied. The assertion that HURAIN is deceptively similar to HORAIN is based upon an artificial letter-by-letter comparison. The Applicant’s mark must be considered in its entirety and overall commercial impression. The Applicant’s HURAIN mark has an independently created presentation, logo, stylization and trade identity. Furthermore, the Applicant’s adoption in 2015 predates the Opponent’s claimed adoption in 2019.

REPLY TO PARA 24:

The contents are denied. The Opponent has failed to identify any actual instance of confusion, deception, mistaken purchase, complaint or passing off resulting from the Applicant’s use of HURAIN. Mere assertion of possible confusion is insufficient, particularly where the Applicant has been using the mark since 2015.

REPLY TO PARA 25:

The contents are denied. The fact that two marks may contain certain common letters or possess a broadly similar pronunciation does not, by itself, establish deceptive similarity. The marks must be assessed in their entirety and in their actual commercial context. The Applicant’s mark HURAIN is an independently adopted trademark and is not a mere alteration of HORAIN.

REPLY TO PARA 26:

The contents are denied. The Opponent’s argument concerning individual consonants and alleged similarity of pronunciation is artificial and speculative. Trademark comparison cannot be reduced to the alleged interchangeability of individual letters. The overall impression created by the respective marks, their presentation and commercial context must be considered.

REPLY TO PARA 27:

The contents are categorically denied. There has been no copying or intentional appropriation of the Opponent’s trademark. The Applicant’s mark was independently adopted in 2015, whereas the Opponent’s claimed adoption of HORAIN occurred in 2019. Accordingly, the allegation of intentional copying is factually untenable.

REPLY TO PARA 28:

The contents are denied. There is nothing unclear or non-distinctive about HURAIN. HURAIN is the Applicant’s independently adopted trademark and has been used by him since 2015. The Opponent cannot dictate what constitutes the distinctive element of the Applicant’s mark or disregard the Applicant’s own established commercial identity.

REPLY TO PARA 29:

The contents are denied. The Applicant’s mark does not dilute the Opponent’s alleged trademark and was not adopted to capitalize upon any goodwill of the Opponent. The allegation is particularly untenable because the Applicant’s adoption predates the Opponent’s claimed adoption.

REPLY TO PARA 30:

The contents are denied. The Applicant specifically denies copying the Opponent’s colour scheme, pattern, font or packaging. The Applicant’s label, logo, artwork, get-up and overall presentation were independently created/adopted by the Applicant and constitute a separate commercial identity. The Opponent is put to strict proof of the alleged copying of any specific artistic work, packaging, colour scheme, font or get-up.

REPLY TO PARA 31:

The contents are denied. The allegation that the mark is “dominated” by the letters “ASNA” is legally and factually misconceived. The Applicant’s mark is HURAIN, considered as a whole. The Opponent cannot claim exclusive ownership over ordinary/common letters or sounds occurring within a trademark while ignoring the overall differences between the respective marks.

REPLY TO PARA 32:

The contents are denied. The Applicant’s trademark is capable of distinguishing the Applicant’s goods and has in fact been used for that very purpose since 2015. The Applicant denies that the mark was adopted in bad faith or that it is inherently incapable of distinguishing his goods. The statutory provisions cited by the Opponent are therefore not attracted on the facts pleaded.

REPLY TO PARA 33:

The contents are emphatically denied. There has been no intention whatsoever to ride upon the alleged reputation of HORAIN. The allegation of subsidiary, parallel or low-cost version is entirely speculative. The Applicant’s independent use since 2015 establishes that HURAIN is associated with the Applicant’s own business and not with the Opponent.

REPLY TO PARA 34:

The contents are denied. The Opponent cannot claim an unrestricted monopoly over every mark which it considers similar. The Applicant’s HURAIN is his independently adopted mark and has acquired its own identity through longstanding use. No actual evidence of fraud, deception or confusion has been pleaded or produced.

REPLY TO PARA 35:

The contents are denied. The Opponent has failed to establish that the Applicant’s mark falls within the statutory prohibition relied upon. The mere fact that the goods fall in the same class does not by itself determine the issue of deceptive similarity or proprietorship. The Applicant’s independent adoption and use since 2015 is a material circumstance which the Opponent has failed to properly address.

REPLY TO PARA 36:

The contents are denied. The Opponent cannot claim that it is the prior adopter of the disputed commercial identity when its own pleading places its adoption of HORAIN in 2019, whereas the Applicant’s HURAIN was adopted and used since 2015. The Applicant therefore has an independent and bona fide basis for seeking registration of his mark.

REPLY TO PARA 37:

The contents are denied. There is no infringement of the Opponent’s proprietary rights. The Applicant is not using the Opponent’s trademark but his own independently adopted trademark HURAIN. The alleged infringement is therefore denied both factually and legally.

REPLY TO PARA 38:

The contents are categorically denied. The allegation of mala fide intention is false and unsupported by evidence. The Applicant has not adopted HURAIN to benefit from the goodwill of the Opponent. On the contrary, the Applicant’s claimed use from 2015 substantially predates the Opponent’s claimed adoption of HORAIN in 2019.

REPLY TO PARA 39:

The contents are denied. There is no absolute principle that registration of one trademark prevents every subsequently registered mark having some phonetic or visual resemblance. Each case must be determined on its own facts, including the respective marks, goods, presentation, circumstances of adoption, actual use, reputation and likelihood of confusion. In the present case, the Applicant’s independent prior use since 2015 is a material distinguishing circumstance.

REPLY TO PARA 40:

The contents are denied. There is no reasonable likelihood that consumers would believe that HURAIN originates from, is sponsored by, affiliated with or endorsed by the Opponent merely because the marks are alleged to have some phonetic resemblance. The Opponent has not identified any actual instance of confusion or deception.

REPLY TO PARA 41:

The contents are denied. The Opponent’s assertion that HORAIN COSMETICS is a “well-known” trademark is specifically denied unless and until established by competent evidence. A claim of well-known status cannot be established merely by repeating the expression “well-known” in a Notice of Opposition. The Opponent is put to strict proof of the alleged fame, duration, geographical extent, sales, advertising, market recognition and other factors necessary to substantiate such claim.

REPLY TO PARA 42:

The contents are denied. The alleged loss of sales, dilution and brand erosion are speculative and unsupported. The Opponent has not established any causal connection between the Applicant’s bona fide use of HURAIN and any alleged damage to the Opponent.

REPLY TO PARA 43:

The contents are denied. The alleged cause of action is misconceived. The Applicant’s HURAIN was independently adopted and used from 2015. The Opponent’s own case places its claimed adoption of HORAIN in 2019. Therefore, the allegation that the Applicant subsequently adopted HURAIN to take advantage of the Opponent’s goodwill is contrary to the chronology relied upon by the Opponent itself.

REPLY TO PARA 44:

The contents are denied. The Opponent has failed to establish any statutory ground sufficient to refuse Application No. 747260. The Applicant accordingly reiterates that HURAIN is his own independently adopted, bona fide and longstanding trademark and is entitled to proceed to registration.

The Applicant further submits that HURAIN was independently adopted in 2015, long before the Opponent’s claimed adoption of HORAIN COSMETICS in 2019. The Applicant has continuously and commercially used HURAIN since 2015 in connection with his cosmetics business.

There was no knowledge, intention or attempt on the part of the Applicant to appropriate the Opponent’s alleged goodwill. The Applicant has developed his own trade identity, packaging, label, logo, artistic presentation and get-up in connection with HURAIN.

The Opponent is put to strict proof of its alleged extensive reputation, turnover, advertising expenditure, market share, consumer recognition and alleged well-known status.

The Opponent has not identified any specific consumer, transaction, complaint, returned product, misdirected order or other concrete incident demonstrating actual confusion arising from the Applicant’s use of HURAIN.

The Opponent cannot obtain an unrestricted monopoly over every word having an alleged phonetic resemblance to HORAIN, particularly where the Applicant establishes independent prior adoption and use.

The Applicant has honestly invested time, resources and goodwill in developing HURAIN since 2015. The Applicant should not be deprived of registration on the basis of an unsupported allegation that his independently adopted mark was copied from a later-adopted trademark.

In view of the foregoing, it is respectfully prayed that Opposition No. 3499/2025 may kindly be dismissed; the Notice of Opposition may kindly be rejected; Application No. 747260 in Class 03 for registration of the trademark HURAIN may kindly be allowed to proceed in accordance with law; the Applicant may kindly be recognized as the bona fide and independent adopter and proprietor of HURAIN; the Applicant’s continuous use of HURAIN since 2015, pr

یہ معاہدہ مورخہ 1 اگست 2026  مابین درج ذیل  فریقین  طے پایا:

فریقِ اوّل/مالک دکان :
احتشام الدین صدیقی، CNIC نمبر 3740511273275، ساکن ترلائ کلاں اسلام اباد۔

فریقِ دوم/کیئر ٹیکر:
جناب ظہیر عثمان ولد محمد فیروز، CNIC نمبر 16202-0930810-9، ساکن قولیار، ڈاکخانہ ڈھرنال، تحصیل فتح جنگ، ضلع اٹک۔

جائیداد

فریقِ اوّل کی ملکیتی دکان نمبر FF-11 عقیق ہائٹس، فرسٹ فلور، سروس/کورنگ روڈ، پاکستان ٹاؤن، فیز-I، اسلام آباد فریقِ دوم کو بطور کیئر ٹیکر/نگران سپرد کی جاتی ہیں۔

شرائط و اختیارات

1. فریقِ دوم کو مذکورہ دکانوں کے لیے مناسب کرایہ دار تلاش کرنے اور مالک کی منظوری و مشاورت سے کرایہ پر دینے کا اختیار ہوگا۔
2. فریقِ دوم مالک کی جانب سے کرایہ دار کے ساتھ Rent Agreement کرنے اور کرایہ داری سے متعلق ضروری دستاویزی کارروائی مکمل کرنے کا مجاز ہوگا۔
3. فریقِ دوم دکان خالی ہونے کی صورت میں قبضہ واپس لینے، چابیاں حاصل کرنے اور دکان کی حفاظت کا ذمہ دار ہوگا۔
4. فریقِ دوم دکانوں سے متعلق تمام معاملات میں پلازہ مینجمنٹ سے ضروری مشاورت کرے گا اور پلازہ کے قواعد و ضوابط کی پابندی یقینی بنائے گا۔
5. دکانوں کی ملکیت، کرایہ اور دیگر مالی حقوق بدستور فریقِ اوّل کے ہوں گے۔ فریقِ دوم کو کسی قسم کا ملکیتی حق حاصل نہیں ہوگا۔
6. فریقِ دوم کرایہ، سیکیورٹی اور دیگر وصولیوں کا مکمل حساب رکھے گا اور مالک کو بروقت جمع/حساب فراہم کرے گا۔
7. فریقِ دوم مالک کی پیشگی اجازت کے بغیر دکان کو خود استعمال، منتقل، سب لیز یا کسی تیسرے شخص کے حوالے نہیں کرے گا۔
8. فریقین کو اختیار ہو گا کہ وہ ایک ماہ کے نوٹس پر  یہ معاہدہ  ختم کر سکتے ہیں، اور خاتمہ کی صورت میں فریقِ دوم تمام چابیاں، ریکارڈ اور متعلقہ دستاویزات مالک کے حوالے کرے گا۔

دونوں فریقین نے مندرجہ بالا شرائط کو پڑھ/سمجھ کر اپنی آزاد مرضی سے قبول کیا اور بطور ثبوت دستخط کیے۔

8/25/26, 10:55 AM - Brandex: AFFIDAVIT

I, IMRAN MAQBOOL, trading as SHEIKH FOOD INDUSTRIES, having business address at Canal Road, Sanyal Pull, Tandlianwala, Faisalabad, Pakistan, do hereby solemnly affirm and declare as under:

1. That I am the proprietor of SHEIKH FOOD INDUSTRIES and am fully competent and authorized to swear this affidavit.

2. That I have applied for registration of the trademark “DOUBLE A.1 FOODS” under Application No. 573246 (complete Application No. 1/T/0001/573246) in Class 30, in respect of “SPICES INCLUDING HALDI POWDER, DHANIA POWDER AND RED CHILLI POWDER.”

3. That the aforesaid trademark application was filed on 03 July 2020.

4. That the trademark “DOUBLE A.1 FOODS” was already in bona fide commercial use by me in Pakistan since the year 2019, prior to the filing of the aforesaid application.

5. That the said trademark has been used in relation to the aforesaid goods through product packaging, labels and commercial sales in the ordinary course of business.

6. That, in response to the requirement of the Learned Trademark Examiner regarding evidence supporting the claimed period of use prior to the filing of the application, I am submitting copies/specimens of the product packaging bearing the trademark “DOUBLE A.1 FOODS” and showing manufacturing and expiry dates from the year 2019.

7. That the aforesaid packaging/specimens constitute genuine documentary evidence of the commercial use of the trademark “DOUBLE A.1 FOODS” in relation to the claimed goods since 2019.

8. That the use of the trademark has been bona fide and in the ordinary course of trade and was not merely undertaken for the purpose of obtaining registration.

9. That the documents and specimens submitted along with this affidavit are true and genuine copies of packaging/products used and offered for sale by me under the aforesaid trademark.

10. That the contents of this affidavit are true and correct to the best of my knowledge and belief and nothing material has been concealed therefrom.

DEPONENT

IMRAN MAQBOOL
Trading as SHEIKH FOOD INDUSTRIES

VERIFICATION

Verified on oath at ____________ on this ___ day of __________, 2026, that the contents of paragraphs 1 to 10 above are true and correct to the best of my knowledge and belief and nothing material has been concealed therefrom.

IMRAN MAQBOOL

9/11/26, 4:30 PM - Brandex: REPLY TO LEGAL NOTICE

WITHOUT PREJUDICE / WITHOUT ADMISSION OF LIABILITY

To:
M/s Abdullah Attorneys
Advocates, Consultants, IP Matters
Faisalabad, Pakistan

Subject: REPLY TO LEGAL NOTICE DATED 19.08.2026 ISSUED ON BEHALF OF MR. ABDUL SATTAR SHAH / M/S YADGAR SWEETS & BAKERS

Dear Sirs,

Under instructions and on behalf of our client Mr. Sheikh Ahsan Ali, trading as YADGAR MASALA CHAKKI / YADGAR SPICES / Super Store, Okara, we hereby respond to your legal notice dated 19.08.2026.

At the outset, the allegations of trademark infringement, copyright infringement, deceptive similarity, copying and unlawful conduct contained in your notice are specifically denied, except to the extent expressly admitted herein. Your notice contains broad allegations without providing the registration particulars, certificates, representations of the alleged protected works/marks, or sufficient particulars of the material allegedly infringed by our client.

1. Our client is independently and bona fide carrying on his business and is using the mark/name “YADGAR” together with his own specific, distinctive and independently created logo, artistic representation, get-up, design and overall commercial presentation. Our client's artistic representation and business identity are his own and are neither a ditto copy nor a reproduction of the logo, artwork, trade dress or get-up of your client's sweets and bakery business.

2. It is specifically denied that our client has copied or reproduced any alleged artistic work belonging to your client. A mere allegation that two businesses use a common word does not, by itself, establish copying of an artistic work. For a copyright allegation, the particular copyrighted work allegedly infringed and the allegedly copied elements must first be clearly identified and compared with our client's independently created work.

3. Your client is stated in your notice to be conducting business as M/s YADGAR SWEETS & BAKERS, Faisalabad, whereas our client's commercial activities relate to a super store/grocery and spices business, including spices and other grocery/food products. The nature, presentation, business model and overall commercial identity of the respective businesses therefore require proper consideration rather than treating the mere presence of the word “YADGAR” as conclusive infringement.

4. Your assertion appears to proceed on the assumption that registration of a mark containing the word “YADGAR” automatically confers an unrestricted monopoly against every use of that word irrespective of its representation, composite elements, registered specification, relevant goods/services, market context and surrounding circumstances. Such a proposition is denied. The actual scope of any statutory right must necessarily be determined by the precise mark registered, its representation, class, specification of goods/services, limitations/disclaimers (if any), and the applicable law.

5. Our client's mark must therefore be considered as a whole, including its particular design, device, stylisation, presentation and commercial context. Your notice does not provide any meaningful side-by-side comparison demonstrating that our client's complete mark, logo or artistic work is identical with or copied from your client's alleged registered mark/artistic work.

6. Your notice repeatedly alleges that our client's material is “similar”, “deceptively similar”, a “ditto copy” and “identical”. These are serious allegations; however, the notice fails to identify with reasonable particularity which exact logo, artwork, social-media publication, publicity board, packaging or other material is alleged to constitute infringement, which protected elements are said to have been copied, and how the respective marks/business presentations are allegedly likely to cause deception or confusion.

7. Your notice further does not disclose the basic particulars necessary for our client to properly evaluate your assertions, including the trademark application/registration number(s), relevant class(es), exact specification of goods/services, filing/registration dates, registered representation of the mark, copyright registration number(s), title/representation of the alleged copyrighted artistic work and the corresponding registration certificate(s).

8. Furthermore, trademark and copyright constitute distinct legal rights governed by different statutory requirements and tests. Your notice broadly combines allegations concerning trademark, copyright, logo, calligraphy, design, colour scheme and other alleged rights without precisely identifying which particular registered right is said to be infringed by which particular act of our client.

9. The generalized references in your notice to criminal liability, the Pakistan Penal Code and FIA proceedings are also denied insofar as they are asserted against our client without specifying the particular statutory offence, ingredients thereof and factual basis allegedly attracting such criminal jurisdiction. Our client is conducting a bona fide commercial business under his independently adopted and created business identity and strongly denies having committed any illegal or criminal act.

10. Our client has neither sought to represent himself as your client nor claimed any association, sponsorship, authorization or business connection with YADGAR SWEETS & BAKERS. Likewise, our client has no intention of appropriating your client's goodwill, logo or artistic work. His business identity and design have been independently created and are being used for his own commercial activities.

11. Accordingly, the sweeping demand that our client cease every use of “YADGAR”, without first establishing the precise scope and particulars of your client's alleged statutory rights and the alleged infringing use, is not admitted and cannot be accepted merely on the basis of the assertions contained in your notice.

REQUEST FOR DOCUMENTS & AMICABLE CLARIFICATION

Notwithstanding the foregoing, our client respects legitimate intellectual property rights and remains willing to examine any genuine concern objectively and professionally.

You are therefore requested to kindly provide us with copies/details of:

1. Trademark registration certificate(s) relied upon by your client, including registration/application number, class, specification and registered representation;


2. Copyright registration certificate(s) together with a clear copy of the artistic work actually deposited/registered;


3. Documentary particulars of the mark/logo/get-up which your client alleges our client has copied;


4. Copies/screenshots/photographs of our client's specific material which you allege constitutes infringement; and


5. Any other registration or statutory right specifically relied upon in your notice.



Upon receipt thereof, the respective marks, artistic works, fields of business, goods/services, overall get-up and commercial presentation can be placed side by side and objectively compared. Such comparison will also enable both parties to properly distinguish your client's sweets/bakery business from our client's super store, grocery and spices business and determine whether any genuine legal issue survives.

Until the above particulars and documents are furnished, the allegations contained in your notice are denied and no admission whatsoever may be inferred from this reply.

Nevertheless, in the interest of professional courtesy and avoidance of unnecessary litigation, our client remains open to an amicable and lawful resolution of any genuine concern after examination of the relevant registrations and competing marks/artistic works.

All legal and equitable rights, remedies and defences of our client are expressly reserved.

Yours faithfully,
COUNSEL FOR MR. SHEIKH AHSAN ALI
YADGAR MASALA CHAKKI / YADGAR SPICES
Okara, Pakistan

Significance

“Purion Pharmaceutical” is a distinctive coined name representing purity, quality and innovation in the pharmaceutical field. The word “Pharmaceutical” clearly indicates the nature and scope of the proposed business activities relating to the development, manufacturing, marketing, and distribution of pharmaceutical and healthcare products.
We request that the second proposed name PURION PHARMACEUTICAL (SMC-PRIVATE) LIMITED may kindly be considered and approved.
